  data (freelist link overwrites only the low u16) and FULL pages
  (where the live population sits) are unreachable from the active
  list. peek LIVE (full-heap magic+cls scan, free_count-authoritative
  freelist filter) fixed both.

THE ANSWER (mission item 2 - what the peek named):
- c256 (341K live at park / 520K at floor): 98% = ArcInner<BinderTxn>
  {strong=1, weak=1, from_proc=ONE daemon, sequential ids} - every
  oneway txn whose receiver never issues BC_FREE_BUFFER pins its Arc
  via the alloc block (cc54-family services).
- c1024 (8.8K park / 84-95K floor): userva-first = Vec<BufBlock>
  binder-alloc arrays + small = ArcInner<BinderThread> (2.5K at park).
- MMs DO NOT LEAK (MM_NEWS/MM_DROPS counters: live 72@85 procs, 148@
  158, 161@sc244 - tracks procs exactly). The leg-16 leak-via-mm
  theory is dead. Total binder-family footprint at the floor ~=210MB
  of 831MB - the mission's 100MB-class lever, twice over.

LEG-18 (launcher line: R-only VMA fd40 write blocker — probe roll) —
OPENED 06:30.

Mission (from 86225d02): the launcher + honydataability crash-loop
writes (lock cmpxchg, a refcount/lock op) into a READ-only file-backed
VMA (fd 40, off 0, 72KB at 0x7ff081ec0000). Ordered leads: (1) DFX
probe roll to name fd 40's path + open mode at fault time and every
mmap/mprotect EACCES; (2) if the fd WAS O_RDWR and asked PROT_WRITE
yet the VMA came back R-only, audit VMA flag construction.

PRE-ROLL AUDIT RESULT (code reading, no roll needed):
- VmaFlags bits decoded: 0x1 READ, 0x2 WRITE, 0x4 EXEC, 0x8 SHARED,
  0x10 PRIVATE. The faulting VMA flags=0x11 = READ|PRIVATE (leg-17's
  "R-only SHARED" shorthand was wrong on the SHARED bit: it is a
  PRIVATE mapping). The rip module VMA 0x15 = R|X|PRIVATE.
- sys_mmap_inner constructs VmaFlags exactly from (prot, map_flags) —
  WRITE iff PROT_WRITE, no silent downgrade; EACCES paths return
  errors, they never create a downgraded VMA.
- MAP_FIXED replacement surgery preserves overlapped pieces' flags
  verbatim (mm_ops.rs x86_64 mmap); mprotect rewrites only on request;
  fork copies flags; binder translate_fd_into and unix-socket
  SCM_RIGHTS install the SAME Arc<File> (open mode preserved);
  memfd_create is always O_RDWR; ashmem_mmap builds SharedMemory/
  Anonymous VMA types, NOT FileBacked — so the fd-40 VMA came from the
  GENERIC file path, i.e. an actual mmap(PROT_READ, MAP_PRIVATE) call
  succeeded there. Leading theory: the app's intended RW|SHARED mmap
  failed (EACCES: read-only fd) and a read-only fallback mapping
  stayed; probes decide.
- LOCATION VERIFIED: 6ba58562 (binder reclaim, the ~210MB floor lever)
  is on feature/samgr-callback ONLY, NOT in main — the leg-17
  close-out line "main's merged binder fix 6ba58562" is wrong; it is a
  branch commit / mainline candidate.

PROBES LANDED (commit 4554da7a): MMAP-EACCES + MPROTECT-EACCES
attribution lines (fd/path/accmode/prot/flags), APPMAPSRC broadened to
every family file-backed mmap with accmode (budget 150), USEGV-FOR
cr2fd line (fault VMA's fd path/raw flags/inode mode+size + same-fd
sibling VMAs + vma_files-pin fallback), nearvma dump now prints the 12
entries NEAREST cr2 (sc251B's ascending walk burned the budget 2.5MB
below the fault), full-forensics budget 6->8.

sc252 ROLLING (launched 06:24): usbless oh-img-p, fresh scgold reflink
userdata-sc252.img, quietcon LOGLEVEL=4 dfx=schb,sleepsnap, SMP2 KVM
4G, SECS=1200, HDCPORT=12108, shotmon 30s. QEMU pid in

1. R80 orphan-watch CADENCE BUG confirmed: confirmation required a
   second sighting 50..60 jiffies after the first (re-record rule

   are binder rekicks (REKICK_SECS=15 = 1500 jiffies at HZ=100). The
   R80 LIVE-phantom heal could NEVER mature while CPUs stayed busy.
   sc252/253: zero R80 heals vs 1359/2050 leak lines.

   census tids are pol=0 (SCHED_NORMAL), dl_thr=0, and wok>=1 with the
   task still parked: the kicks DO wake them (wake->link->run->recheck-
   cond->re-park loop inside wait_event_interruptible; read_wait_since
   is stored once per ENTERING binder read, so the census cannot tell a
   re-parked cycler from a stranded task). The census shape is mostly
   HEALTHY cycling, not wake loss.

- R81: orphan_watch gains an exec-clock word; confirmation = same
  (task,pid) sighting >=threshold jiffies apart with sum_exec_runtime
  FROZEN (a task executing anywhere is charged by update_curr every
  tick; frozen exec = provably not running, at ANY observer cadence).
  An advancing exec clock refreshes the record (replaces the 60-jiffy
  re-record rule). Direct-console DL-ENQ-DEFERRED print (R82) on the
  only silent sleeping-wake refusal.
- WAKE-RUN-LEAK census: +pol/on_rq/dl_thr and per-episode kick outcome
  counters (wok/wno; wake_thread records wake_up result; reset at
  park).

  stall_report's WORLD walk retires undelivered txns (pick_j==0) older
  than 30s oneway / 60s sync via the node-death pattern
  (set_holds_async_slot(false) + dead_reply_to_sender for sync +
  finish_work; async-slot handoff mirrors sweep_stale_async). Frees the

LAUNCHER CRASH NAMED (sc254 full dump, pid 1630 honydataability ==
pid 1635 m.ohos.launcher, same rip 0x3fbf1187d2, same cr2+0xa93c):
- vma(cr2) 0x7ff081bc0000-0x7ff081bd2000 flags=0x11 FileBacked fd=40
  off=0x0; cr2fd: path=anon_inode accmode=0x2 (O_RDWR) rawflags=0x2.
- Siblings of fd 40: off=0x11000 R|X, off=0x37000 R, off=0x38000 RW,
  plus a second whole-file R map at 0x7ff08c200000. => fd 40 is a
  MEMFD-backed dlopen'd library; the fault VMA is its FIRST PT_LOAD
  (R-only, 72KB); rip module 0x3fbf077000 (fd closed, off=0x76000) is
  the same module family's text at the fixed base.
- ripbytes f0 0f b1 51 10 ... = lock cmpxchg [rcx+0x10],edx; refcount
  op writing at PT_LOAD0+0xa93c (R-only). The lock should live in
  writable memory; relocations/pointer point into the rodata segment.
- NEXT DECIDER (sc255, rolling): per-comm APPMAPSRC budget (48/comm;
  the appspawn daemon ate the global 150 before any launcher instance
  exec'd in sc254) + USEGV-L one-liner now prints fdVMA fd/path/acc/
  off/flags at ANY budget depth + per-comm full-dump budget (first 2
  per comm). Scenarios: (a) ld.so requested PROT_READ for the segment
  containing +0xa93c and the memfd ELF itself puts a lock in rodata ->
  the memfd CONTENT was corrupted at write time (our write path? the
  module generator?) or the generator is layout-broken only under our
  ABI; (b) the app mapped an RW twin we lost (no evidence yet); (c)
  relocations resolved against the wrong of the TWO mappings of the
  same file (0x7ff081bc0000 vs fixed-base 0x3fbf077000 vs second
  whole-file map 0x7ff08c200000).

LAUNCHER CRASH SITE FULLY NAMED (offline forensics, no new roll needed):
- The rip pattern f0 0f b1 51 10 85 c0 74 15 ff 45 ec 48 89 71 18
  occurs at EXACTLY ONE offset in the whole system image: file offset
  0x1177d2 of /system/lib/ld-musl-x86_64.so.1 (raw grep + debugfs
  icheck/ncheck -> inode 2447). Load bias = 0x3fbf000000: the rip VMA
  (0x3fbf077000, off=0x76000) is ld-musl's own PT_LOAD1. THE CRASHING
  CODE IS THE DYNAMIC LINKER ITSELF.
- Disassembly: fault = pthread_cond_timedwait+0x620 (0x1187d2), the
  a_cas(w->state, WAITING, SIGNALED) claim step of the waiter-queue
  walk in the apply() helper at 0x118700 (callers: signal/broadcast/
  leaver paths at 116f44/116fd4/118018/118198).
- OHOS musl waiter node: struct waiter { link@0, link@8, state@0x10,
  barrier@0x14, ret@0x18 }, STACK-ALLOCATED at rbp-0x50 of the waiting
  thread (enqueue at 0x1182d4). Queue fields live in the condvar:
  tail@c+0x8, waiters@c+0xc, seq@c+0x10, lock@c+0x20, head@c+0x28.
  rcx at the fault = *(c+0x28) = _c_head; rdi-0x20 gives c =
  0x7ff081bfc108 = the memfd module's .bss+0x108 (global condvar).
- So _c_head = module_base+0xa92c = INSIDE the module's R-only first
  PT_LOAD. Enqueue only ever stores waiter STACK addresses => the head
  is a STALE pointer: the node address stopped being a stack and became
  the module's rodata (unmap + dlopen reuse of the hole), or the .bss
  page is a dirty recycled anon page carrying the OLD instance's
  corrupted condvar (fresh bss must be zero — dirty recycle = kernel
  mm bug). Every appspawn fork inherits condvar+module at the same
  base => identical crash in every child. render_service's memcpy twin
  (guard-page read of 0x3a8 bytes) = same stale-per-thread-state
  family. NOT a VMA-construction bug: the mapping faithfully follows
  the file's phdrs; the pointer is wrong on the userspace side.
- EACCES/read-only-fd lead: CLOSED for good (fd 40 was O_RDWR memfd;
  zero MMAP/MPROTECT-EACCES in every roll).

PROBES LANDED (bb274328, x86_64 trap.rs, dfx-gated): cr2elf (module's
own ELF header+phdrs via page-table read of its offset-0 mapping; tags
the PT_LOAD covering the fault file offset), cr2bytes (32B around cr2),
cr2strs (.dynstr names the module even for anon memfd), frame (rbp-0x58
deref: this frame parks the condvar pointer there; dumps the condvar's
own words — stale-head vs whole-condvar-garbage decider).

QUIETCON DISCOVERY: sleep_snapshot prints at pr_info; ALL sc25x rolls
ran loglevel 4 => the wedge's parked-syscall call graph was loglevel-
filtered in every roll so far (the "sleepsnap never fires" mystery).
sc257 (same recipe as sc254, probes aboard) wedged pre-spawn at the
usual ~t290s: 2208 WAKE-RUN-LEAK lines all wok>=kicks wno=0 (healthy
cycling shapes, conversations frozen QUIET — FXRS global count ~3700
ops in 25min rules out CPU-pegging spinners for this class), SAREG

IApplicationStateObserver registration to pids 434/411(mmi_service)/509

THREAD 1 (launcher crash-loop) — sc258's KILLED ROLL HELD THE FULL
DECIDER DATASET (read offline, no new roll needed for the first cut):
- 4 identical launcher crashes (pids 2061/2139/2699/3041), all
  m.ohos.launcher, rip=ld-musl+0x1187d2, SAME cr2/rsp/rbp every time.
- cr2fd-pin NAMES THE MODULE: /mnt/sandbox/system/system/lib64/
  chipset-sdk-sp/libhicollie.z.so (memfd fd 40). Extracted from
  system.img (inode 2543); phdrs match the cr2elf dump exactly.
- cr2bytes: the fault target page is CORRECT module rodata (strings
  readable). Not a dirty target page.
- Full musl apply() decode (disassembled the image's ld-musl): fault =
  lock cmpxchg [w+0x10] in the waiter walk, w = rcx = *(c+0x28) =
  _c_head, c = r9 = module+0x3c108, r15 = c-0x118 = module+0x3bff0.
- libhicollie disassembly: 0x3bff0 is the watchdog thread's C++ object
  (function-local static; guard byte 0x3c388; __cxa_atexit dtor).
  Layout: mutex@+0xf0, pthread_cond_t@+0x118 (the crash condvar),
  strings at +0x20/+0xc8/+0x148, deadline field +0x308. The watchdog
  thread (pthread_setname'd at entry 0x1ac81) loops clockwait on
  cond@+0x118.
- THE HEAD VALUE IS A STRING POINTER: module+0xa92c is EXACTLY the
  first byte of the .rodata literal "thread DfxWatchdog: Current
  scenario is task name: " (used at 0x1a864 to build the watchdog event
  string). _c_head can legally hold only 0 or a waiter STACK address —
  never module rodata.
- GEOMETRIC KILL of the stale-waiter/dlclose-reuse family: a waiter
  node is stack-allocated (musl enqueue: rbp-0x50); for head to equal
  module_base+0xa92c, a stack must have covered that VA; but any
  process generation that owns this condvar has libhicollie mapped at
  that same deterministic base (fork inheritance), covering the VA with
  the R-only ph1. In-process dlclose+re-dlopen moves the base but also
  DESTROYS the .bss carrying the stale head. No stale-waiter sequence
  is geometrically consistent. Also killed: kernel-futex wild write
  (kernel/src/sync/futex.rs has ZERO user-memory writes).
- SURVIVING candidates: (a) KERNEL dirty anon-page recycle — the .bss
  page arrives carrying a previous process's bytes (constructor ran
  over most fields, but the condvar/mutex/never-written globals keep
  the stale pattern); (b) userspace wild write into obj+0x140 in an
  appspawn ancestor, inherited COW by every launcher child (4 identical
  crashes = one corruption, fork-inherited).
- DECIDER PROBES LANDED (x86_64 trap.rs, dfx-gated apply()-shape):
  cv (condvar words via r9: tail/waiters/seq/lock/head), obj (watchdog
  object fields), bssslack (THE SMOKING GUN: ph4 memsz ends at
  module+0x3c51c; the anon bss VMA spans +0x3c000..+0x40000; slack
  0x3c520..+0x3d000 and the whole +0x3d000 page are addressed by
  NOTHING in the module — any nonzero word there = the page arrived
  dirty from the recycler = kernel bug).
- INFRA NOTE: run/oh-img symlink was re-pointed (01:37, cross-lane)
  to a broken perf-audit path and killed the ramdisk-x64.img link;
  repointed to ui-sprint's intact copy (md5-matched oh-display's).

LEG-22 ROLLS AND VERDICT.

sc259 (unpatched, LOGLEVEL=7, probes): the deciders landed on a live
instance (honydataability pid 1683, apply()-shape):
- cv via r9: +0x8 tail == +0x28 head == 0x7ff09b051680 (ONE waiter on
  a fork-COW'd stack; the "pagefault: cow" line at w+0x10 right before
  the fatal one = the walk's CAS succeeding on the dead waiter),
  +0x10 seq=1, +0x20 lock=1 (held by the crashing apply itself).
- Crash regs: rbx=0xffffffff — the walk ran with nwaiters=-1 (the
  clockwait TIMEOUT dequeue mode, which follows the whole chain), CAS'd
  the real waiter, then followed w->next = 0x7ff08290a92c (module rodata
  string) and faulted writing w->state there.
- obj fields around the condvar: sane constructor values (deadline
  +0x308 = real ms). bssslack (page 0x3c000 tail past memsz): ALL ZERO
  — the condvar's own page is clean.
- bssslack2 (page module+0x3d000, beyond ph4 memsz 0x3c51c, addressed
  by nothing in the module): NONZERO — string fragments + pointer
  0x7ff083e1eb30. DIRTY ANON PAGE HANDED OUT BY THE RECYCLER.
  => KERNEL BUG #2 confirmed, independent of the launcher crash.
  HANDOFF (mm lane): the page recycler/allocator gives out dirty pages
  for fresh anon mappings somewhere between free and alloc.

MUSL DECODE (image's ld-musl-x86_64.so.1, disassembled):
- pthread_cond_timedwait entry DOES zero the node (movaps at 0x1181da,
  rbp-0x50..-0x30) — the old "enqueue never inits next" first cut was
  wrong at the micro level. The poison is LATER:
- The queue's next links are sound while threads live, BUT a fork
  child inherits the condvar WITH the parent's queued waiter node on
  the parent's (now dead) thread stack (COW). POSIX: condvars with
  waiters are UB across fork; OHOS appspawn forks without exec, so
  every child inherits hicollie's watchdog condvar in that state.
- The child's own threads then REUSE the dead stack (musl stack cache
  hands the same VA to a new thread); the new thread's frames write
  the "thread DfxWatchdog: Current scenario..." literal pointer
  (materialized at 0x1a864 in the event-string builder) into the dead
  node's next slot. The next timeout/signal walk follows it into the
  R-only ph1 → SIGSEGV. The walk's fixup (head = last walked node,
  0x118830) also propagates garbage into c->head — the sc258 shape
  (head = string directly).
- KERNEL VERDICT: SEGV delivery is CORRECT; the launcher crash-loop is
  a userspace (image) bug family. Kernel-side dirty-recycle is real
  but orthogonal (bssslack2).

IMAGE PATCH (patch-ldmusl-zeronext.sh): zeros the waiter node's next
link at enqueue (movq $0,(%rcx) via two .text pad caves, hijack at
0x1182ea). Collapses the in-process garbage-next family. Applied to
this worktree's private system.img (raw bmap'd block writes, inode
2447; image copy verified byte-identical to the patched build).

sc260 (patched, same recipe): 30 min roll.
- Launcher crash-loop DEAD: 1 condvar crash total (pid 4695, t~25min —
  the fork+stack-reuse residual variant, which enqueue-zeroing cannot
  cover by design) vs sc258's 4-in-15min loop. Launcher instances
  stay alive (STALLWATCH: tid 1281 parked >90s in a futex/clock poll,
  NOT crash-cycling; later instance 4695 alive to the tail).
- The other 12 SEGVs: 8x usb_host NULL (known noise) + 4 shutdown-tail
  OS_IPC worker crashes (same family as sc258/259 tails).
- OnForeground/launcher.ready: still NOT reached. The live blocker is
  now the Account chain: "AccountDelegateNormalImpl:
  QueryForegroundUserId: GetForegroundOsAccountLocalId failed, status:
  4194349" — the launcher parks in a futex/clock retry loop waiting on
  the osa/account services (the sc257 SAREG census shape: registration
  re-sends with no replies). Screenshot stays the GPU probe gradient.
- NEXT for this lane: (1) the account/osa IPC chain (why
  GetForegroundOsAccountLocalId fails — status 4194349), (2) the
  fork-residual condvar variant needs a musl-side fork-time condvar
  reinit (pthread_atfork in libhicollie init, or an image patch on
  fork) — next leg's call, (3) mm lane: dirty anon page recycle.

Session close: probes (cv/obj/bssslack/bssslack2) committed; patch
script committed; forensics (hicollie.asm, ld-musl disassembly,

LEG-23 (OH UI lane, leg-22 successor) — OPENED. Mission: the account
chain (QueryForegroundUserId 4194349).

OFFLINE FORENSICS (sc260 log + image binaries, no roll needed) — THE
4194349 DECODE, END TO END:
- The printer of "AccountDelegateNormalImpl: QueryForegroundUserId" is
  DISTRIBUTEDDATA (libdistributeddatasvc.z.so — the ONLY image file
  containing that string, inode 3072; DistributedData's own account
  delegate, NOT appexecfwk's). All 88 sc260 prints are the
  distributeddata family retry loop (~5s), NOT the launcher process.
- Client: OsAccountManager::GetForegroundOsAccountLocalId -> OsAccount::
  GetForegroundOsAccountLocalId (libos_account_innerkits.z.so 0x3bd10):
  null proxy -> 0x400012; proxy vcall -> OsAccountProxy::GetForeground-
  OsAccountLocalId (0x5a670): SendRequest tx code **0x4d** (SAREG does
  NOT see it — its code filter is {3,4,7,10,21}), then reply.ReadInt32
  = status. 4194349 = 0x40002d was read FROM THE REPLY PARCEL.
- Server (libaccountmgr.z.so, OsAccountManagerService::GetForeground-
  OsAccountLocalId 0x1943aa): appId = IPCSkeleton::GetCallingUid()/1000;
  inner->IsOsAccountForeground(appId) [vt+0x1e0 @0x1564ae] = membership
  lookup in the container at inner+0x168 (activated/foreground set,
  written by SendMsgForAccountActivate @0x15dd9c); miss -> 0x400026;
  fallback inner->[vt+0x1d8](0) miss -> maps 0x400026 to **0x40002d**.
- VERDICT: the IPC reply LANDS (fast, well-formed domain error, not a
  kernel loss and not a no-reply). The value means "calling app is in
  no activated/foreground account". The blocker is UPSTREAM: the
  activated/foreground set at inner+0x168 stays empty in sc260-class
  boots — either account activation never completes, or the kernel
  reports a callingUid that maps to no account. Deciders for sc261:
  (a) binder trace events on main: tx 0x4d timeline + caller uid;
  (b) accountmgr activation logs (OnStateChanged state 7 family);
  (c) whether the IAccount code=4 "unanswered" SAREG sends are oneway
  (no reply expected — probe cannot distinguish) or real losses.
- CAUTION for the lane: sc217's lucky boot had getOsAccountLocalId
  (a different call) succeed; that does NOT prove the foreground map
  was populated.

LEG-23 ROLL-1 (sc261) + THE ACCOUNT-CHAIN VERDICT.

ACCOUNT CHAIN: RESOLVED (no kernel bug; the handoff's premise was a
misread of the error storm):
- The 88 "QueryForegroundUserId ... 4194349" lines are DISTRIBUTEDDATA's
  retry loop (not the launcher), and they all sit in a ~20s window
  (t~60-80s): accountmgr starts t~55s, publishes SA:200, init runs, and
  ActivateDefaultOsAccount (PrepareForDefaultAccount ->
  SendMsgForAccountActivate -> SetParameter("bootevent.account.ready",
  "true")) completes at t~80s — sc260 line 46738 PCTL-SEND pid=891
  key=bootevent.account.ready true, AFTER the last error (46364).
  From then on the launcher's own queries succeed: "LauncherAbility-
  Manager --> getOsAccountLocalId localId 100" repeats to the log tail.
  The 4194349 storm is the pre-activation window (foreground set at
  inner+0x168 empty -> IsOsAccountForeground miss 0x400026 -> fallback
  miss -> 0x40002d). POSIX-clean: replies land, values are domain
  errors. sc212's t~1083s "OnStateChanged state 7" was the same
  activation, just slower.
- IPC transport itself healthy for this chain: tx 0x4d send+reply fast.
  The "unanswered" SAREG code=4 IAccount sends need the oneway bit to
  adjudicate (trace events now on main can do it next roll if it
  matters).

THE REAL KILLER OF sc258/259/260 (found in all three tails): KERNEL
PANIC inside the user-SEGV forensics — "PANIC: attempt to subtract
with overflow, trap.rs:2340". The leg-21 rbp-locals probe computed
regs.rbp - 0x58 on a fault frame with rbp=0xb (pid 526's adjudicated
ld-musl rep-movsq crash). The debug overflow check fired inside the
page-fault handler and took the kernel down. leg-22's "4 shutdown-tail
OS_IPC worker crashes (same family as sc258/259 tails)" WAS this
panic trigger — every roll in this family died at the first tail
crash, so nothing past it was ever observed.
FIX (1d5a6744): rbp window only opens for canonical user rbp
(wrapping_sub), stack/rbp word walks checked_add, r15+0x118 shape
compare wrapping_add. Probe output formats unchanged. The fault is
now delivered to the task instead of killing the kernel.

LEG-23 ROLL-1 VERDICT (sc261, completed 07:52 after the predecessor
died; second attempt picked it up mid-run) + KERNEL BUG #3 FIXED.

sc261 OUTCOME (45 min roll, patched ld-musl image, survived to the
timeout for the first time in this family):
- The account chain held: bootevent.account.ready set early (line
  45914, ActivateDefaultOsAccount at 41652), and the launcher's own
  queries succeed ("getOsAccountLocalId localId 100" to the tail).
- The launcher crash-loop stayed dead: exactly ONE SIGSEGV leader
  death (pid 1413, line 100454) — the fork-residual condvar variant
  leg-22 predicted: the timeout-walk CAS'd a waiter node at
  0x7ff08204a92c+0x10 that landed inside the child's OWN binder
  buffer mapping (base 0x7ff082006000, fd=40 anon_inode, flags 0x11
  R-only) inherited stale in the condvar queue at fork (DONTFORK
  drops the parent's mapping; the child's binder mmap re-covered the
  VA read-only). rbx=0xffffffff (timeout dequeue), ripbytes = LOCK
  CMPXCHG [rcx+0x10]. Enqueue-zeroing cannot cover nodes inherited
  FROM the parent's queue — needs the fork-time condvar reinit
  (musl-side pthread_atfork or image patch); still open, ONE crash
  per 45 min is survivable.
- KERNEL BUG #3 (fix 73e2ad1e): pid 1421's exit_group ->
  kill_other_threads -> kill_dump_tgid page-faulted the KERNEL (no
  fixup, "kernel fault addr=0x13fd0 rip=<u64 as LowerHex>::fmt
  entry") while formatting an EXITED sibling's saved syscall: the
  thread's kernel stack was already back in the stack cache
  (kernel_stack_bottom zeroed at task.rs free path) while the PINNED
  Task survived; kernel_stack_top() = 0x14000 and &regs.orig_rax =
  0x14000 - 0xa8 + 0x78 = 0x13fd0 EXACTLY. The SAFETY comments
  ("the kernel stack stays mapped while the Task lives") were wrong.
  Fix: Task::owns_kernel_stack() (bottom != 0) gating every
  foreign-task entry-frame reader — kill_dump_tgid (the crash site),
  the APPPK12 walk, apppark_rets, rwfz_stack_frames, both RWFRZ
  censuses (futex.rs), holder_forensics + owner-chase,
  sleep_snapshot, user_park_stack_dump (binder.rs). x86_64+riscv64
  build. NOTE: the boot did NOT die at the KERNPANIC — the other
  CPU kept running (149k log lines after), but 1 CPU down =
  degraded; the fix removes the fault entirely.
- The post-fault tail: KILLDUMP cap (8/boot) was already spent, so
  no dump took the risk again; the system limped on 1 CPU.

THE LIVE BLOCKER after all of the above: the AMS LIFECYCLE_TIMEOUT
restart loop. Every launcher instance dies the same death: spawn ->
AbilityScheduler attach ("AtomicServiceAbilityManager getInstance",
ProcessSkeleton AttachObject desc:*.AbilityScheduler) -> SILENCE ->
LIFECYCLE_HALF_TIMEOUT -> LIFECYCLE_TIMEOUT ("load" phase) -> AMS
"restart ability" -> respawn. The same load-phase timeout hits
settingsdata's DataExtAbility. The parked launcher looks HEALTHY at
kill time (KILLDUMP tgid 1413: main thread poll(2), OS_IPC_1/2 in
binder ioctl read on /dev/binder fd6 — a textbook IDLE app waiting
for binder work). hilog logs NO ability-schedule event at all, so
"AMS never sent ScheduleLoadAbility" vs "sent but the wake/read was
lost in the kernel" is indistinguishable from the log alone.
DECIDER for sc262 (600e9be9): SLTX tracer (binder_transaction
enqueue: latch launcher tgid by comm, print every txn to/from it,
500/boot) + SLRD (binder_thread_read: every txn the launcher's own
threads actually read, 600/boot). in-without-read = kernel wake/read
loss; no-in-at-all = AMS-side stall (trace AMS next).

sc262 ROLLING: new kernel (merge 859f2adc + fixes 73e2ad1e, 600e9be9),
same recipe (oh-img-p patched system, fresh scgold reflink userdata,
KVM SMP2 4G, loglevel=7 dfx=schb,sleepsnap, SECS=2700, shotmon on).

LEG-23 ROLL-2 (sc262) + THE LIFECYCLE_TIMEOUT CHAIN, END TO END.

sc262 (same recipe as sc261 + the new kernel: merge 859f2adc, entry-
frame guard 73e2ad1e, SLTX/SLRD v1 tracers 600e9be9): ZERO KERNPANIC
(sc261 had the 0x13fd0 one), ZERO launcher leader SIGSEGVs mid-run

THE ADJUDICATION THE TRACERS BOUGHT (v1 data + APPTXN + wakewatch):
- AMS DOES send the load: "SLTX in pid=801 -> 329 code=0x7 oneway=true
  dlen=2364-4672" (IAbilityScheduler ScheduleLoadAbility; also code
  0x5 Schedule*Transaction, 93 sends in the first window) — the sends
  land at enqueue with parcel token ohos.appexec.. (28 chars).
- APPTXN (320 lines): EVERY child-aimed delivery routes
  route=child-looper with a direct thread wake (post_txn_to_child_
  thread + wake_ths) — the sc161-168 kernel chain (shared proc +
  reg_tgid routing + looper-only targeting + per-tgid SPAWN_LOOPER +
  (ptr,reg_tgid) node identity) all still holds.
- No binder-stall ever fired (no reader parked >120s; the 15s re-kick
  heal had nothing to heal). The launcher MAIN thread is ALIVE —
  STALLWATCH rings show clock_gettime/futex/writev loops (not a park),
  and its own alog narration runs (getOsAccountLocalId localId 100
  pairs, LauncherAbilityManager/CommonBundleManager).
- WHY THE LOAD NEVER FINISHES, then: hilog's own DFX_BlockMonitor
  names the killer — three DETERMINISTIC multi-second binder round
  trips per boot, same three in sc261 AND sc262:
    SendRequest cost 3038-3177ms code=1  desc:*.IDisplayComposer
    SendRequest cost 4188-6496ms code=1034 desc:*.AbilityManager
    SendRequest cost 2090-2070ms code=14  desc:*.IOsAccount
  (plus XCollie BlockMonitor "GetOsAccountLocalIdFromProcessInner
  Duration 2070ms" — the launcher's JS init loop burns ~2s PER
  osAccount query; the ability-load sequence contains several such
  hops and cannot fit the LIFECYCLE window).
- The kernel-side source of seconds-long latencies: WAKE-STRANDED
  outliers — 140 wakes/boot (>1s each, max 26986ms on pid 305) while
  the cumulative wake path is healthy (15.4M wakes, avg <1ms). The
  strand reports show incumbent tasks with preempt_count=1/2 (pid
  318, a thread of early service 315, pc=1 in EVERY strand line) —
  the wakewatch header's own "vsync-starvation family" (cc13: a 16ms
  usleep takes 0.5-0.7s, one ~41s dead stop per boot). The launcher
  LIFECYCLE_TIMEOUT is this family biting the app-start path.
- TRACER V1 FLAWS, FIXED IN v2 (f4c7d54a): appspawn children NEVER
  open /dev/binder (76 opens, zero child pids — they send through the
  daemon's INHERITED fd; proc.pid == the daemon for every child), so
  v1's latch caught the daemon's whole proc and burned 500 lines in
  ~5 min; the read side was worse (OS_IPC/OS_FFRT are generic worker
  names — samgr alone ate 337/600 — and tgid==proc.pid can never hold
  for children on the inherited fd). v2 gates on the tgid LEADER's
  comm (m.ohos./os./com.), prints sender tgid + target node reg_tgid,
  budgets 1500/boot each side. NEXT ROLL (sc263, armed) pairs
  "SLTX in child=X" with "SLRD tgid=X read" for the final delivery
  confirmation, + dfx=memwatch for the restart-loop leak curve.

STRAND-REPORT CONTAMINANT (documented for every future reader of
WAKE-STRANDED lines): the h-slot for the PICKING cpu reads the slot's
prev DURING __schedule's pick — prev legally carries the picking
context's own GRQ guard (acquired sched.rs:2152, released :2283 AFTER
the pick), so that slot's pc=1 is an ARTIFACT BY CONSTRUCTION (and the
v3 tp print reads the same task pre-switch — tp pc matches for the
same reason). Only the OTHER cpu's h-slot is a clean incumbent read.
The genuinely-leaked incumbent evidence stands on the other-slot reads
(pc=2/pc=258) and the PREEMPT-LEAK/PARKED-LEAK census.

pong stamps (hop1 = wake syscall + side1 pick; hop2 = side0 pick) to

LEG-26 PASS 2 — MINIMIZATION + PER-HOP + GEOMETRY + OWNERSHIP (the
standalone mechanism NAMED, 10 more probe boots srA-srH/srE-srH):
- Minimization (sweep2, 10 cells): pong+pipe+fork@SMP2 = REPRO
  (1194ms); +fork4 = 1304ms; full-mix = 1576ms; pong+pipe WITHOUT
  fork = 52ms CLEAN; fork-only = 314ms; SMP1 = 163ms. FORK CHURN +
  BOTH blocking-IPC families + SMP2 is the minimal trigger; the
  borderline cells all sit in a 700-1000ms band (same degradation,
  >1s crossing is stochastic). Repro stability: every srA-config boot
  (7/7) showed the >250ms class; verdict REPRO on most.
- Per-hop pong stamps: hop1 (wake+peer-pick) and hop2 (own pick) are
  FAST (us) in the biggest stalls — the time sits between the peer's
  resume and its next move: a RUNNING USERSPACE THREAD descheduled
  ~1s mid-turn, i.e. runnable-task starvation, pure.
- Ran-ring dumps at strand time: BOTH CPUs rotate frantically among
  the same cohort (8 distinct tasks per ring, all @0j), tick counters
  advancing. NOT idle-harvest failure, NOT monopoly, NOT tick loss.
- Geometry: at pick time every victim is vr=0/lag=0 (leftmost, at
  min); last-skip table: most victims NEVER skipped (no ownership
  screen ever refused them); ownership words CLEAN (on_cpu=0,
  claim=-1, pk=LIVE), no slot, no SWITCHING_OUT names them.
- Enqueue-time geometry: EVERY stranded victim entered the queue
  +29..39ms of virtual runtime AHEAD of min (enq_lag=0).
=> THE MINIMAL-REPRO STALL CLASS IS FAIR-QUEUE WAIT AMPLIFICATION:
  the victim's accumulated vruntime lead (~35ms) is worked off under
  ~10x oversubscription (16 wakeable threads / 2 TCG CPUs + fork
  churn) => 300-400ms typical, >1s tail. Marks, ticks, monopolies all
  clean. Linux running this shape shows the same math; wakeup
  preemption exists and correctly does not fire for an AHEAD task.
  The >250ms SREPRO class on the minimal rootfs is NOT the OH
  pathology — it is the benign cousin.
- PARKED-LEAK (pc=1 on parked waiters) + STALE-CLAIM-RESET (26-42/
  boot) reproduce in exactly the stalling cells — the ownership/pc
  corruption SEEDS are present, but srL1 (leakhunt) REPRO'd with ZERO
  parked-leak: not necessary for the tail.
OH SIDE (sc290/sc291, lvl4 plain KVM SMP2, dfx=wwcensus[+leakhunt]):
- sc290: LIFECYCLE_TIMEOUT loop at full rate (182 in ~20 min); OH
  WAKE-STRANDED lines name the incumbents with RAW pc=1/2 OUTSIDE
  tick context = GENUINE leaked PREEMPT_OFFSET bits: h0=(316/pc=1)
  repeat, h1=(1/pc=1) INIT ITSELF, 328/pc=2, 492, 577/pc=2...
  victims strand 1-3.7s behind them. THE OH PRODUCER IS THE LEAKED-pc
  INCUMBENT FAMILY (init included), as leg-25 suspected; the
  minimal-rootfs workload does not form them (needs OH's userspace).
- STALL-HOG fired on init and m.ohos.launcher with pc=0x10000 — my
  probe's raw-pc bug (tick's own in-flight offset), fixed in-tree
  (masked base/nestx/pctick); their 100j monopolies were clean-pc
  userspace busy loops.
